From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.81, F50.0, F50.01\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s my eating. Mostly at night. Once I start, it feels like I’m watching myself do it, telling myself to stop, but I just keep reaching for more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can't control yourself or stop eating once you start these nighttime eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… once I start, it’s hard to stop. I’ll be telling myself I should quit, but I just keep reaching for more anyway.\n\nDoctor: When you have these nighttime eating episodes, do you find yourself eating a lot more than most people would in the same amount of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Some nights I’ll start with leftovers, and then it turns into sandwiches, cereal, cookies… whatever’s around. It’s more than I’d ever sit down and eat for a normal dinner.\n\nDoctor: Do these nighttime eating episodes bother you or cause problems in your life, like with your health, relationships, or how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does. I mean, I still get to work, keep up with what I have to do. But I’ve been avoiding things I used to do, like the theater or my walking group. I just feel… embarrassed, I guess, and stuck thinking about whether I look disciplined enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating an unusually large amount of food during these nighttime episodes compared to what most people would eat in a similar time period?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it is unusually large. Like, I’ll start with leftovers, and then it’s sandwiches, cereal, cookies… whatever snacks are in the pantry. It’s definitely more than I’d ever put on a plate for supper.\n\nDoctor: Do you tend to eat a lot more food overall at night compared to what you eat during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… at night it’s a lot more. During the day I might not eat that much, but some evenings I start with leftovers and then it turns into sandwiches, cereal, cookies… whatever’s in the pantry. It’s more than a normal supper, for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating until you feel uncomfortably full during these nighttime eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t really pay attention to it that way. Sometimes I feel heavy afterward, I guess, but I don’t know if I’d say uncomfortably full.\n\nDoctor: Has your nighttime eating caused you to feel depressed, anxious, or significantly upset? Or has it affected your ability to function in your daily life, like at work, socially, or at home?\n\nPatient: I’m still getting through work and the usual stuff at home. It hasn’t… stopped me that way.\n\nBut I have been backing out of things, like the theater group and walking club. I get kind of ashamed, and then I’m thinking too much about whether I look like I have any discipline. So I just don’t go sometimes.\n\nDoctor: During these nighttime eating episodes, do you feel like you're not in control of your eating, like you can't stop once you start?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s probably the main thing. Once I start at night, I can sort of see what I’m doing and I’m telling myself to stop, but… I just keep reaching for more anyway.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you eat a lot more at night compared to what you typically eat during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say so. Some nights it’s way more than I’d eat during the day, or for a normal dinner. I’ll start with leftovers and then it turns into sandwiches, cereal, cookies… whatever’s around. It’s not something I’d put on one plate.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or impaired in any area of your life specifically because you feel like you can't control your eating at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still doing my job and keeping up with the basic stuff.\n\nBut it does get to me. I’ve been saying no to things like theater or the walking club because I feel embarrassed… like people can tell I don’t have control or discipline. So I just avoid it sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do these nighttime eating episodes usually happen after you’ve already gone to bed and woke up, or do they happen more in the evening while you’re still up?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate it. It’s usually later, after I’ve been up in the evening… but I don’t really keep track of whether I’d already gone to bed or not.\n\nDoctor: Do these nighttime eating episodes usually happen after you wake up during the night, like after you’ve already fallen asleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, sometimes I’m up late anyway, and I don’t always remember if I’d actually fallen asleep first or not. It’s kind of blurry.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually start eating during the night after you’ve already fallen asleep and then woke back up?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I don’t really keep track of whether I woke up first or was just still awake late. It’s kind of hard for me to pin down.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually start eating during the night after you’ve already fallen asleep and then woke back up, or are you typically still awake when you start eating?\n\nPatient: I honestly can’t tell. Sometimes I’m just still up, I think… other times I might’ve dozed off first. Some nights I’ll start with leftovers, and then it turns into sandwiches, cereal, cookies… whatever’s around. I mean, I still get to work, keep up with what I have to do. But I’ve been avoiding things I used to do, like the theater or my walking group. I just feel… embarrassed, I guess, and stuck thinking about whether I look disciplined enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating an unusually large amount of food during these nighttime episodes compared to what most people would eat in a similar time period?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it is unusually large. Like, I’ll start with leftovers, and then it’s sandwiches, cereal, cookies… whatever snacks are in the pantry. It’s definitely more than I’d ever put on a plate for supper.\n\nDoctor: Do you tend to eat a lot more food overall at night compared to what you eat during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… at night it’s a lot more. It’s more than a normal supper, for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating until you feel uncomfortably full during these nighttime eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t really pay attention to it that way. Sometimes I feel heavy afterward, I guess, but I don’t know if I’d say uncomfortably full.\n\nDoctor: Has your nighttime eating caused you to feel depressed, anxious, or significantly upset? Or has it affected your ability to function in your daily life, like at work, socially, or at home?\n\nPatient: I’m still getting through work and the usual stuff at home. It hasn’t… stopped me that way.\n\nBut I have been backing out of things, like the theater group and walking club. I get kind of ashamed, and then I’m thinking too much about whether I look like I have any discipline. So I just don’t go sometimes.\n\nDoctor: During these nighttime eating episodes, do you feel like you're not in control of your eating, like you can't stop once you start?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s probably the main thing. Once I start at night, I can sort of see what I’m doing and I’m telling myself to stop, but… I just keep reaching for more anyway.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you eat a lot more at night compared to what you typically eat during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say so. Some nights it’s way more than I’d eat during the day, or for a normal dinner. I’ll start with leftovers and then it turns into sandwiches, cereal, cookies… whatever’s around. It’s not something I’d put on one plate.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or impaired in any area of your life specifically because you feel like you can't control your eating at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still doing my job and keeping up with the basic stuff.\n\nBut it does get to me. I’ve been saying no to things like theater or the walking club because I feel embarrassed… like people can tell I don’t have control or discipline. So I just avoid it sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do these nighttime eating episodes usually happen after you’ve already gone to bed and woke up, or do they happen more in the evening while you’re still up?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate it. It’s usually later, after I’ve been up in the evening… but I don’t really keep track of whether I’d already gone to bed or not.\n\nDoctor: Do these nighttime eating episodes usually happen after you wake up during the night, like after you’ve already fallen asleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, sometimes I’m up late anyway, and I don’t always remember if I’d actually fallen asleep first or not. It’s kind of blurry.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually start eating during the night after you’ve already fallen asleep and then woke back up?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I don’t think of it that clearly… whether I’d actually fallen asleep first or just stayed up too late. It kind of runs together.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself eating a lot of food after you wake up in the middle of the night?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say often. I don’t really keep track of whether I woke up first or was just still awake late. It’s kind of hard for me to pin down.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually start eating during the night after you’ve already fallen asleep and then woke back up, or are you typically still awake when you start eating?\n\nPatient: I honestly can’t tell. Sometimes I’m just still up, I think… other times I might’ve dozed off first. I don’t really pay attention to it that way.\n\nDoctor: Has your nighttime eating caused any problems with your physical health, mood, or ability to concentrate and get things done during the day?\n\nPatient: I mean… I still get to work and handle what I have to. But I’ve been avoiding some things, like going to the theater or meeting the walking club. I just feel embarrassed, I guess, and caught up in whether I seem… disciplined enough.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a recent time when you ate a lot at night—were you already asleep and then woke up before you started eating, or were you still awake when you started eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. The last time… it was late, and I remember being in the kitchen, but I can’t say for sure if I’d already fallen asleep first or if I was just still up. I wasn’t paying attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Can you remember if your nighttime eating episodes tend to start after you wake up in the middle of the night, rather than when you're just staying up late and still awake?\n\nPatient: I really don’t know. I haven’t kept track of whether I’d woken up first or was just still awake late. It all kind of runs together.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It’s not something I’d put on one plate.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or impaired in any area of your life specifically because you feel like you can't control your eating at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still doing my job and keeping up with the basic stuff.\n\nBut it does get to me. I’ve been saying no to things like theater or the walking club because I feel embarrassed… like people can tell I don’t have control or discipline. So I just avoid it sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do these nighttime eating episodes usually happen after you’ve already gone to bed and woke up, or do they happen more in the evening while you’re still up?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate it. It’s usually later, after I’ve been up in the evening… but I don’t really keep track of whether I’d already gone to bed or not.\n\nDoctor: Do these nighttime eating episodes usually happen after you wake up during the night, like after you’ve already fallen asleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, sometimes I’m up late anyway, and I don’t always remember if I’d actually fallen asleep first or not. It’s kind of blurry.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually start eating during the night after you’ve already fallen asleep and then woke back up?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I don’t think of it that clearly… whether I’d actually fallen asleep first or just stayed up too late. It kind of runs together.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself eating a lot of food after you wake up in the middle of the night?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say often. I don’t really keep track of whether I woke up first or was just still awake late. It’s kind of hard for me to pin down.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually start eating during the night after you’ve already fallen asleep and then woke back up, or are you typically still awake when you start eating?\n\nPatient: I honestly can’t tell. Sometimes I’m just still up, I think… other times I might’ve dozed off first. I don’t really pay attention to it that way.\n\nDoctor: Has your nighttime eating caused any problems with your physical health, mood, or ability to concentrate and get things done during the day?\n\nPatient: I mean… I still get to work and handle what I have to. But I’ve been avoiding some things, like going to the theater or meeting the walking club. I just feel embarrassed, I guess, and caught up in whether I seem… disciplined enough.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a recent time when you ate a lot at night—were you already asleep and then woke up before you started eating, or were you still awake when you started eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I wasn’t paying attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Can you remember if your nighttime eating episodes tend to start after you wake up in the middle of the night, rather than when you're just staying up late and still awake?\n\nPatient: I really don’t know. I haven’t kept track of whether I’d woken up first or was just still awake late. It all kind of runs together.\n\nDoctor: Has your nighttime eating ever made it hard for you to focus at work, affected your physical health, or caused you to feel depressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Not really with work.
